Hmm, actually I need to reconsider the mechanism more carefully.

In GAPDH catalysis:
- Cys-S⁻ is the nucleophile (needs to be deprotonated)
- His-ImH⁺ acts as general acid to protonate the leaving group

So activity requires:
- Cys to be deprotonated (favored at pH &gt; pKa_Cys)
- His to be protonated (favored at pH &lt; pKa_His)

The overlap of these two requirements creates the bell curve:
- Below pH ~pKa_His: His is protonated ✓ but Cys is also protonated ✗ (actually Cys pKa is higher, so Cys should still be protonated at low pH too)
- At intermediate pH: His is protonated ✓ and Cys is deprotonated ✓ → activity
- Above pH ~pKa_His: His loses proton ✗ → activity decreases

Wait, this doesn&#x27;t quite work because if Cys pKa &gt; His pKa, then the Cys wouldn&#x27;t be deprotonated until above the His pKa, creating a narrow window.

Actually, in GAPDH, the environment lowers the Cys pKa significantly (to around 7-8) while the His pKa is around 6-7. The exact pKa values depend on the enzyme environment.

Let me reconsider with pKa_Cys ~8 and pKa_His ~6:
- At pH 6: Cys protonated (no activity), His protonated (would be good if Cys worked)
- At pH 7: Cys mostly protonated (partial activity), His mostly protonated
- At pH 8: Cys mostly deprotonated (good), His mostly deprotonated (loses general acid function)
- At pH 9: Cys fully deprotonated, His fully deprotonated (no general acid)

This would give a peak around pH 7-8, with the acidic limb governed by Cys protonation and the alkaline limb governed by His deprotonation.

If pKa_Cys = 7 and pKa_His = 6:
- Peak would be around pH 7-7.5
- Acidic limb (Cys protonation): half activity around pH 6 (half of Cys deprotonated)
- Alkaline limb (His deprotonation): half activity around pH 7 (half of His deprotonated)

But that gives a very narrow curve. In reality, the pH dependence is more complex with multiple ionizable groups contributing.

For actual GAPDH enzymes:
- Most have optima between 8.0 and 9.5
- The pH profiles are typically broad with half-maximum points spanning about 2-3 pH units total

I think the answer is B, as GAPDH from thermophilic organisms typically has:
- Optimal pH around 8.3
- Reasonable half-activity points at pH 6.6 and 9.6

This gives a total span of 3.0 pH units between half-activity points, which is reasonable for an enzyme with multiple ionizable groups affecting activity.

For GAPDH from various organisms:
- E. coli GAPDH: optimum pH ~8.5-9.0
- Yeast GAPDH: optimum pH ~8.5-9.0
- Rabbit muscle GAPDH: optimum pH ~8.5-9.0
- Thermus caldophilus GAPDH: optimum pH ~8.5-9.0
- Thermotoga maritima GAPDH: optimum pH ~8.3-9.0

Given the thermophilic-like characteristics of this sequence (high charged residue content, compact structure), this is likely from a thermophilic organism.
